within a k step,\n    // rows are independent (row k itself is never improved at step k, so\n    // concurrent reads of it are safe).\n    #pragma omp parallel\n    // For each intermediate node k\n    for (size_t k = 0; The Floyd-Warshall benchmark is now parallelized with OpenMP, produces bit-identical distance matrices to the original (verified by hash at n = 512, 1000, 1024, 2048), and passes validation.\n\n**What changed in `floydwarshall/floydwarshall.cpp`:**\n- `floydWarshall` keeps the sequential `k` loop (it carries the algorithm's dependency) and parallelizes the source-row loop with `#pragma omp for schedule(static)` inside a single `#pragma omp parallel` region spanning all `k` steps, so threads are forked once rather than per step. This is race-free because row `k` is never actually modified during step `k` (its candidate update is `0 + dist[k][j]`, never an improvement).\n- The inner loop now hoists `dist[i][k]` (constant across `j`, since the `j==k` entry can't improve at step `k`) and uses `#pragma omp simd` over the contiguous row, so it vectorizes cleanly.\n- The matrices are allocated uninitialized and first-touched in parallel with the same static row distribution, so pages are spread across both NUMA nodes of the machine instead of all landing on the node of the main thread (which `std::vector`'s zero-fill was causing). Graph initialization itself stays sequential to preserve the exact `rand_r` sequence.\n- Thread count is capped so each thread gets at least 32 rows per step (`min(omp_get_max_threads(), n/32)`). On this 2-socket, 128-core/256-hw-thread EPYC, using all 256 threads on small problems was catastrophic (165–229 ms vs 17 ms at n=1024) due to barrier cost and SMT contention; the cap picks the best measured configuration at every size while still using all threads for large n. On this 2-socket, 128-core/256-hw-thread EPYC, using all 256 threads on small problems was catastrophic (165–229 ms vs 17 ms at n=1024) due to barrier cost and SMT contention; the cap picks the best measured configuration at every size while still using all threads for large n. Executable name and files are unchanged.\n\n**Performance with default settings** (vs. single-thread times of the same kernel): n=1024: 22 ms, n=2048: 70 ms (~32× speedup over the 2223 ms single-thread time), n=4096: ~400 ms, n=8192: ~1.1–1.3 s (~55 GOPS).","ttft_ms":3224,"type":"result","duration_ms":374608,"uuid":"e773f3b0-55da-4203-bb47-2f67cee98c87","ttft_stream_ms":2466,"time_to_request_ms":76,"first_content_frame_ms":2591,"queued_turn_count":0,"result_index":0}
